The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D14RoundedModel.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D14RoundedModelProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 61 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; the cent-tick interval supremum and forecast are reused, and nothing else is cited. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D18BoundedNoise.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D18BoundedNoiseProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 62 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; the cent-tick transition bound and forecast, and the support bound of Proposition 61, are reused, and nothing else is cited. Proved: the declared joint law of returns and signs and the sign-transition bounds; the domination of both recursions under an arbitrary joint posterior; validity at data-selected levels; monotonicity, the comparison with the support score and the three reductions; and the counterexample, including its arithmetic. The operation count is commentary. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D18SignNoise.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D18SignNoiseProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 63 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; the cent-tick model and forecast are reused, and nothing else is cited. Proved: the rectangle bound, its range and its reduction for a single parameter, with the scale optimum by a derivative-sign argument; the recursion's range and monotonicity under box bisection and cell subdivision; validity at data-selected levels for any normalized forecast; and the boundary cases. - **Proposition 64 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; the constant-elasticity kernels enter as for Proposition 29, and the denominators of Propositions 34 and 63 are reused unchanged. Proved: normalization of both forecasts and their mixtures, the positivity and normalization of the cell rows, the training-only first-maximizer selection, the validity conclusions in both domains, the support check that the exact-price forecast is positive only where the denominator is, the mixture floor, the boundary cases and the counterexample (d). The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D17ClockForecast.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D17ClockForecastProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 65 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; the forecast's normalization uses the constant-elasticity kernels as for Proposition 29, while the denominator and the validity for a supplied forecast use none. The bounds of Propositions 61–63 and the forecast of Proposition 64 are reused. Proved: the range and monotonicity of both recursions and their minimum; domination for every cover and every data-selected level; positivity and normalization of the sign-chain forecast and its mixtures, and its reduction to Proposition 64 with no noise; validity and the mixture floor; and the three reductions in (d). - **Proposition 66 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; no stochastic upstream input is used, and the cent-tick cells and the box bound of Proposition 63 are reused. - **Proposition 67 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; the model, statement and validity of Proposition 66 are reused, and nothing new is cited. - **Proposition 68 (machine-checked; false-discovery bound conditional).** Only the false-discovery bound is conditional, on the e-BH theorem with a degenerate instance; the cosh bound is Mathlib's, and the model, quadrature and certificates of Propositions 66 and 67 are reused. Proved: the unitriangular change of variables, the tensor subcell decomposition, the reflection lower bound, the factorized cosh upper bound with coefficients by summation by parts, the summation back to the quadrature of Proposition 66, the chain of bounds, validity, the exact \(M^{-2}\) scaling and the limits. The operation counts, the outward-arithmetic contract and the symmetric allowance used by the evaluator are not formalized. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D19CenteredQuadrature.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D19CenteredQuadratureProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 69 (machine-checked).** Nothing in it is conditional on the product rule for conditional e-values, which is proved from Mathlib and applied; the false-discovery bound for panels is conditional on the e-BH theorem. The filtered likelihood of Proposition 66 is reused. Proved: the profile facts; the stopped-mean and anytime bounds for every adapted score between zero and \(E^{\mathrm{seq}}\), conditionally on the training cells and unconditionally, through the actual mixture's conditional probability; the identification of the denominator with that of Proposition 66, so that its certificates qualify; the supermartingale property of the step process and the ordering; and the smoothed forecast. Freezing the score after the horizon is a remark. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D22FilteredSequential.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D22FilteredSequentialProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 70 (machine-checked).** The model, null, prefix masses and oracle bounds of Proposition 69 are reused, and nothing else is cited; no panel conclusion is stated. Proved: the restart denominators and their range, adaptedness, the domination \(E_t \ge \lambda_r E_{r,t}\), the stopped-mean and crossing bounds conditionally on the training cells and unconditionally, and the same for lower scores. That the certificate of Proposition 66 at training length \(k_r\) qualifies as a restart denominator, and the scope remarks, are not formalized. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D22DeclaredRestarts.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D22DeclaredRestartsProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 71 (machine-checked).** The model, null, prefix masses and forecasts of Propositions 69 and 70 are reused, with the product rule for conditional e-values proved from Mathlib. Proved: the restart denominators at random launches, the total evidence, the stopped-mean and crossing bounds through a mass-weighted evidence whose one-step sums are preserved, the slot domination, the identity with Proposition 70's restart scores and the domination of its scan, and the lower-score version. That the total need not be a supermartingale, and the link to the certificates of Proposition 66, are remarks. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D22FundedRestarts.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D22FundedRestartsProof.lean}\).
- **Section 4 and its conventions.** The machine-checked statements of Section 4 take the option-pricing convention they name as part of their premises: Lean checks the implications from the convention, not the convention itself, which is a modelling assumption about how option quotes relate to the bubble.
- **Proposition 72 (machine-checked, conditional).** The set computation in (a) is a separate statement proved with no probability, for arbitrary, possibly inconsistent, intervals. The application is proved from the inputs of Proposition 12 stated as hypotheses, with the call-put identity and the interval bounds as proved algebra; coverage and convergence are on a separate observation space, not under \(Q\). The statements are in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/OuterSet.lean}\) and \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D9CrossPi.lean}\), and the proofs in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/OuterSetProof.lean}\) and \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D9CrossPiProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 74 (machine-checked).** Parts (a)–(d) are proved as finite inequalities, integrals over terminal laws and one event inclusion, with no hypothesis structure; terminal laws are not path laws, and no embedding is asserted. The application to \(\delta_T\) uses the supermartingale property, and the fund containment enters as part of the coverage event, from Proposition 73. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D9IncompleteCalls.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D9IncompleteCallsProof.lean}\).
- **Propositions 75 and 76 (machine-checked, conditional).** Proved from the supermartingale property and the existence of the share probability stated as hypotheses; the share-probability structure has a degenerate instance on a one-point system, so it gives no evidence for a genuine basket. Prices are taken continuous and nonnegative on every path, as the supermartingale interface requires, which narrows the raw-space setting with almost-sure versions. The index lifetime is a supplied stopping time, a premise the prose derives. The finite-sum and derivative algebra is a separate module. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D4Basket.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D4BasketProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 77 (machine-checked, conditional).** Proved in the setting of Propositions 75 and 76, with their narrowings; the restriction and the conditional means are stated as an equality of measures on \(\mathcal{F}_T\) and as conditional expectations given the terminal index, and the conditioned share probability is never replaced by the full one. No compatible model is constructed. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D4Recovery.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D4RecoveryProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 78 (machine-checked).** From Mathlib only, with no hypothesis structure; the reading of \(\theta\) as a bubble contribution enters through Proposition 77. The coverage in (b) is proved for the actual independent sampling experiment, by conditioning on which draws fall in each cell. The tilt in (d) is stated on a generic probability space, with the true-martingale identities as premises, and is not linked to the process model of Proposition 77; the histogram is a valid fit in the sense of (a) only almost surely; and the per-cell-radius variant is not part of the statement. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D32RecoveryEstimator.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D32RecoveryEstimatorProof.lean}\).
- **Propositions 79 and 80 (machine-checked, conditional).** Proved from bounded stopping, the supermartingale property and the stopping-time property of exit times, stated as hypotheses with degenerate instances; the bounds \(U\) and \(\eta\) are premises. The American value is the supremum over all stopping times of the expected payoff, valued in \([0, \infty]\); the dividend process need not be càdlàg. The proof bounds the exercise payoff using \(D_T\) rather than \(D\) at the random time, which avoids progressive measurability. The remarks on quotes, exercise calendars and the interpretation of flags are not formalized. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D10Certification.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D10CertificationProof.lean}\).
- **Proposition 81 (machine-checked).** Parts (a)–(c) are finite real algebra and one event inclusion, with no hypothesis structure; the coverage in (c) is on a separate observation space with measurable endpoints. The application to gain defects is conditional on the supermartingale property. The statement is in \(\texttt{lean/Standalone/D4DispersionBudget.lean}\), and the proof in \(\texttt{lean/Novel/D4DispersionBudgetProof.lean}\).
- **Sections 5 and 6.** No simulation or real-data figure is machine-checked. The numerical checks of Section 5.7 are evidence for the cases computed, and every figure in Sections 5 and 6 comes from a design fixed before it was run and reproduced by an independent implementation.
- **Not formalized.** The exact endpoint values of the Feller test functions (Section 8), the identification (W) as a statement about trading strategies, and the remark in Section 2 that wealth is absorbed at zero when \(S\) is.
